通膨升溫壓抑美股表現，近期聚焦財報結果
2026 年 10 月 15 日
一、 市場動態
1. 科技股領跌，費半連 6 日收黑：美股主要指數 10 月 14 日全數下跌，道瓊工業指數下
跌 0.09%，收 51,621.48 點；S&P 500 指數下跌 1.11%，收 7,718.94 點，為 7 月 29
日以來最大單日跌幅；Nasdaq 綜合指數下跌 1.88%，收 26,692.87 點，為 7 月 23
日以來最大單日跌幅。費城半導體指數下跌 4.30%，收 11,684.38 點，連續第６個交
易日走低，為 2025 年 5 月以來最長連跌紀錄。

2. 類股非全面下挫，資金仍呈輪動現象：半導體及 AI 相關股票在 10/14 跌幅尤其明顯
，AMD 下跌 5.73%、Nvidia 下跌 3.16%、Broadcom 下跌 3.10%，AI 基礎建設相關
的 CoreWeave、Nebius、Oracle 及 Dell 跌幅更深，顯示市場對資本支出規模較大、
融資成本敏感度較高的 AI 相關企業風險偏好明顯下降。不過，S&P 500 的 11 大類
股中有 8 類下跌，資訊科技及通訊服務為主要拖累來源，但醫療保健、必需消費與能
源類股仍逆勢上漲。
3. 美國 10 年期公債殖利率盤中急升後回落：美國 10 年期公債殖利率 10/14 由 5.2%左
右快速升至約 5.33%，逼近近期高點；然而，隨後市場消化 Fed 官員較審慎的政策訊
號，殖利率自高檔回落，終場約收於 5.19%，較前一交易日 5.21%小幅下降約 2 個基
點。
二、 下跌原因分析
1. 美 9 月通膨高於預期，Fed 再升息風險重新升高：美國 10/14 公布 9 月 CPI 月增 0.6%

、年增率也由 8 月的 3.4%上揚至 3.7%；核心 CPI 月增 0.3%、年增率由 8 月的 2.4%
升至 2.6%。其中汽油價格單月大漲 9.1%，貢獻整體 CPI 月增幅超過一半；住宅成本
亦月增 0.4%，顯示物價壓力仍具黏性。此數據公布後，市場重新提高 Fed 進一步升
息的預期，CME FedWatch 所反映的 10 月升息 1 碼機率一度由前一日約 22%提高至
37%。雖然截至 10/15 台北時間早上 7 點左右該機率回降至 29.3%，這也提醒投資人
Fed 的升息路徑仍存在不確定性。
2. 高利率同時壓縮科技股估值與提高 AI 融資成本：科技及半導體公司未來獲利成長占評
價的比重較高，因此對折現率變化相對敏感。當長天期美債殖利率維持 5%以上，市
場願意支付的本益比自然受到限制，即使企業獲利仍維持成長，股價也可能因評價倍
數下降而出現修正。另一方面，本輪 AI 投資需要大量資料中心、伺服器、電力與網路
基礎設施，高利率亦直接增加企業融資成本，反映信用市場對高度依賴外部資金的 AI

基礎建設公司要求更高風險溢酬。

確性不做任何擔保，如有錯漏或疏忽，本行及本行之任何董事或受僱人，毋須負任何法律責任。任何人因信賴此等資料而做出或改變投資決
策，應審慎衡量本身之需求及投資風險，自行承擔一切投資風險並自負盈虧，本行不負任何法律責任。本報告受中華民國著作權法保護為元
大銀行所有，非經本行同意，不得將本報告內容之一部或全部加以複製、轉載或散布。本報告所載述的意見本行可隨時予以更改或撤回，恕
不另行通知。
3. AI 投資焦點由「支出規模」轉向「投資報酬率」:近期市場對 AI 的疑慮逐漸由「需求
是否成長」，轉向「龐大的資本支出能否轉化為足夠營收、獲利與自由現金流」。
Broadcom 執行長陳福陽近期亦指出，AI 未來發展的重要限制可能逐漸由晶片或記憶
體供應轉向資本本身。我們認為，這並不代表 AI 資本支出週期即將結束，而是市場已
由第一階段的「資本支出愈大、成長想像愈高」，逐步進入更重視資本效率與投資報酬
率的第二階段。未來財務結構、現金流與 AI 商業化能力，將成為不同企業評價分化的
重要因素。
4. 中國記憶體競爭疑慮加重半導體短線壓力：中國長鑫存儲（CXMT）10/10 傳出記憶

體技術取得進展，引發市場對未來 DRAM 供給增加及價格競爭的疑慮，記憶體相關類
股率先承壓，並進一步影響整體半導體市場情緒。惟 DRAM 與 GPU、客製化 ASIC、
先進晶圓製程及先進封裝的產業競爭與需求結構並不相同。我們認為，儘管記憶體競
爭升溫值得關注，但目前尚不足以延伸為整體 AI 半導體需求同步轉弱。
5. 高檔籌碼加速獲利了結：AI 與半導體仍是 2026 年美股表現最強勢的投資主題之一，
部分科技股今年累計漲幅已相當可觀。當通膨、殖利率及 AI 投資報酬疑慮同時升高，
先前漲幅較大、投資人部位較集中的族群自然成為優先調節對象。因此，本波費半快
速修正除了總經與基本面疑慮外，亦包含籌碼降溫及風險部位收縮因素，使短期價格
波動幅度可能高於企業基本面實際變化。
三、市場看法
1. 類股輪動明顯，尚非全面看空美股：科技與 AI 相關股票 10 月 14 日明顯承壓，但醫
療保健、必需消費與能源類股仍逆勢上漲，道瓊工業指數僅下跌 0.09%。此外，S&P
500 成分股下跌家數與上漲家數之比約為 1.1：1；相較於指數下跌 1.11%，市場廣度

並未同步大幅惡化。這反映資金主要由高評價且前期漲幅較大的科技與 AI 族群，轉向
現金流較穩定的防禦型產業。現階段市場特徵較接近類股輪動與風格切換，而非無差
別的全面風險撤離，亦尚未看到美股企業獲利或經濟基本面全面反轉的明確訊號。
2. 美股科技股財報前風險降載，Q3 仍存在重新定價空間：近期科技股修正，費城半導
體指數連續６個交易日下跌，也可能部分反映第三季財報季正式展開前，投資人在股
價先前漲幅較大、評價偏高及利率環境不確定下，提前降低風險曝險。真正決定後續
市場方向的關鍵，仍在於實際企業獲利能否支持目前的高成長預期。根據 Bloomberg
季度獲利成長追蹤資料，今年 Q1 與 Q2 財報季期間的 EPS 成長率經驗顯示，隨實際
財報陸續公布，企業獲利成長曾明顯優於財報季開始前的市場預期。儘管這並不保證
Q3 將複製前兩季的上修幅度，但目前市場因財報不確定性所反映的風險折價仍存在
被實際財報重新驗證的可能。若大型科技及半導體公司後續營收、獲利與 AI 需求指引
實際公布結果優於預期，近期風險溢價可望部分收斂；反之，若獲利成長無法跟上目

前評價，市場整理時間則可能進一步延長。

不另行通知。
3. 費半修正幅度大，但 AI 需求尚未出現全面反轉：ASML 在 10/14 所公布的最新第三
季財報反而顯示 AI 相關設備需求仍相當強勁，該公司表示 2027 與 2028 年規劃產能
可能仍無法完全滿足客戶需求，部分客戶亦已表達對 2029 及 2030 年設備需求的興
趣。儘管費城半導體指數近期市場情緒轉弱，但由 ASML 的財報表現來看，目前仍未
看到大型 AI 客戶全面取消晶片訂單、主要雲端服務商同步削減 AI 資本支出，或先進
製程需求快速降溫等結構性訊號。
4. 台積電營收顯示 AI 需求暢旺，留意 10/15 法說會內容：台積電 10/8 公布的第三季營

收達新台幣 1.49 兆元、年增約 50%，創歷史新高，並高於市場原先預估的 1.46 兆元
；9 月單月營收年增 54.6%。強勁的營收表現仍反映 AI 與先進製程需求具有支撐。目
前市場因此呈現「企業營收仍快速成長，但股票評價下降」的狀況。高殖利率環境下
，EPS 上升與本益比下降可以同時發生，後續股價方向將取決於企業獲利上修幅度是
否足以抵銷評價收縮。該公司將於 10/15 下午舉行第三季法人說明會。市場重點將集
中於第四季展望、AI 與 HPC 需求、先進製程、先進封裝及未來資本支出規劃。公司
對 AI 需求與產能投資的最新判斷，將成為判斷近期費半修正主要屬於評價調整，或產
業基本面是否開始出現變化的重要依據。
5. AI 與半導體進入基本面分化階段：我們認為，在高利率環境下，高度仰賴外部融資、
自由現金流尚未成熟的 AI 基礎建設業者，受到的資金成本壓力明顯高於具穩定現金流
的大型科技與雲端服務商。同樣地，記憶體、GPU、ASIC、先進製程及先進封裝各有
不同供需結構。我們預期下一階段 AI 行情將更加重視訂單能見度、產品競爭力、毛利

率、自由現金流及 AI 營收實際兌現，產業內部強弱分化程度可能進一步提高。
6. Fed 路徑仍存不確定性，惟企業獲利可望提供股市支撐：美國 9 月 CPI 升溫使市場重
新評估 Fed 後續升息時點與幅度，美國利率政策仍存在不確定性，長天期美債殖利率
也可能維持高檔。不過，現階段企業獲利成長與 AI 相關需求仍具韌性，若第三季財報

延續前兩季優於預期的趨勢，企業 EPS 若能上修有望部分抵銷高利率對本益比的壓縮
效果。換言之，後續市場表現未必只由 Fed 政策主導，企業實際獲利能否持續兌現，
將是支撐科技與半導體股中期走勢的重要力量。
四、投資建議
1. 短線聚焦美股財報與 Fed 決策，科技股中期趨勢料不致有變：近期市場同時面臨財報
前的觀望態勢、美債殖利率偏高及 AI 資本支出疑慮等不確定因素，短線風險偏好可能
受到壓抑，後續宜關注 10 月美股財報對企業獲利與資本支出展望，以及 FOMC 會議
所釋出的政策訊號。中期而言，目前 AI 相關企業營運與資本支出尚未出現全面轉弱跡

象，科技產業基本面仍具一定支撐，但在利率維持高檔及市場評價偏高的環境下，後
續宜更加重視企業獲利、現金流及資本支出的實際變化。
2. 中天期債券風險報酬仍較均衡：美國 9 月 CPI 升溫使短端殖利率持續受到 Fed 政策路
徑影響，而 20～30 年超長端仍面臨財政赤字、國債供給與期限溢價偏高等結構性壓
力。相較之下，目前殖利率已位於歷史相對高檔，若後續核心通膨或能源價格改善，
中段殖利率具有回落條件，同時其存續期間風險又低於超長端，因此現階段以中天期
美國公債及投資級債的風險報酬結構相對較佳。

[Table_ReportInfo] 2026 年 11 月 16 日      
证 券研究报告•宏观简评报告     
数据点评 
新兴产业与外需支撑景气，内需修复持续承压 
——2026 年 10 月经济数据点评

点评

西 南证券研究院 
[Table_Summary]  摘要：工业生产方面，10 月规模以上工业增加值同比增长 4.8%，增速较上月
回落 0.7 个百分点，低于市场预期。高技术制造业和装备制造业增加值分别增
长 17.6%、10.6%，其中高技术制造业创近五年来单月增速新高，AI相关电子
硬件、工业机器人、3D 打印设备等产品产量保持较快增长。同时，10 月规上
工业出口交货值增长 18.6%，外需仍是工业生产的重要支撑。整体工业生产呈
现总量放缓、结构分化的特征，高技术制造和出口链条景气度较高，传统周期
性行业修复相对滞后。固定资产投资方面，1-10 月全国固定资产投资同比下降
8.1%，降幅较 1-9 月扩大 0.9 个百分点，投资需求仍处于深度收缩区间。基建
投资方面，1-10 月广义基建投资下降 5.1%，专项债发行节奏放缓、资金向实
物工作量转化存在时滞，整体托底效应尚未充分显现，但互联网和相关服务业
投资，以及交通运输、电力供应等重点领域投资仍保持正增长。随着专项债剩
余额度加快使用及“两重”项目建设推进，重点基建领域投资有望获得一定支
撑，但扭转整体投资下行趋势仍面临较大难度。房地产投资方面，1-10 月房地
产开发投资同比下降 21.6%，降幅较 1-9 月扩大 1.0 个百分点，地产仍是整体
投资的重要拖累。供给端施工、竣工面积单月同比分别下降 32.5%、39.1%，
降幅均有所扩大，新开工降幅有所收窄但仍处负增长区间；需求端商品房销售
面积、销售额分别下降 20.7%、20.5%，降幅小幅收窄，但销售修复尚未有效
传导至房企投资和施工活动。房企资金来源继续收缩，行业投资意愿和项目建
设规模仍然偏弱，预计年内地产投资仍处深度调整阶段，降幅收窄时点或推迟
至 2027 年。制造业投资方面，1-10 月制造业投资同比下降 2.9%，降幅较 1-9
月扩大 0.8 个百分点，企业资本开支意愿仍然偏弱。高技术制造业投资增长
3.0%，电子电路、锂离子电池和集成电路制造业投资增速较快，科技产业投资
仍具韧性。“两新”政策继续支撑设备更新，设备工器具购置投资增长 8.9%，
占全部投资比重提升至 20.3%。但汽车、通用设备等装备制造行业投资明显走
弱，反映生产端韧性尚未转化为新增资本开支。消费方面，10 月社会消费品
零售总额同比增长 0.4%，增速较上月回落 0.7 个百分点，消费需求整体偏弱。
汽车类零售同比下降 21.4%，仍是社零增长的主要拖累；通讯器材、金银珠宝
等品类受前期高基数及金价波动影响，零售增速明显回落，家电、家具消费则
受低基数影响边际改善。服务消费相对更具韧性，1-10 月服务零售额增长
4.9%，高于商品零售额 4.0 个百分点。往后看，汽车及地产相关消费能否改善、
以旧换新政策效应能否延续，仍是影响商品消费修复的重要因素，服务消费则
有望继续成为居民消费的重要支撑。 
 工业生产总量边际降温，科技制造与出口链条维持高景气。2026 年 10 月，全
国规模以上工业增加值同比增长 4.8%，增速较上月收窄 0.7 个百分点，低于
wind 一致预期的 5.32%。分行业看，采矿业、制造业、电力、热力、燃气及
水生产和供应业的工业增加值同比较上月均边际走弱，增速分别回落至 0.7%、
5.4%、4.4%。但制造业工业增加值同比相比近十年季节性水平略高，表明生
产端并未出现明显失速。结构上，10 月高技术产业、装备制造业工业增加值
同比分别增长 17.6%、10.6%。高技术产业增速创下近 5 年来单月最高增速，
其中电子工业专用设备制造、计算机外围设备制造、电子器件制造等行业增加
值分别增长 46.8%、44.7%、34.8%，显示电子信息产业链仍处于较高景气区
间。装备制造业 1-10 月累计增加值占全部规模以上工业比重为 37.9%，同比
提高 1.8 个百分点。细分行业中，10 月单月，计算机、通信和其他电子设备制

造业、专用设备制造业、石油和天然气开采业、铁路、船舶、航空航天和其他
运输设备制造业增加值同比分别增长 19.4%、11.5%、9.4%、9.2%，在主要
行业中增幅居前。金属制品业（8.6%）、食品制造业（8.6%）、医药制造业（5.9%）
增速均边际回升。非金属矿物制品业（-2.3%）、煤炭开采和洗选业（-4.4%）
同比降幅均小幅收窄。这反映出工业生产的改善并非全面扩散，传统周期性行
业仍受到需求不足、价格偏弱等因素制约，产业间景气分化较为明显。从产品
看，“人工智能+”带动产业链上下游产量较快增长，既体现在集成电路
（+21.9%）、电子元件（+15.2%）等核心硬件持续扩张，也体现在工业机器人
（+26.0%）、 3D 打印设备（+85.9%）等高端制造设备加快放量，以及地面通
信导航定向设备（+61.5%）等智能终端产品快速增长，反映出 AI产业发展正
由底层硬件向智能制造和多元化应用场景延伸。出口亦对工业生产形成正向带
动。10 月规模以上工业企业出口交货值同比增长 18.6%，增速较上月加快 1.6

个百分点。装备制造业出口交货值增长 20.6%，增速较上月加快 3.4 个百分点，
其中，汽车、电气机械、电子行业出口交货值分别增长 52.2%、23.7%、15.3%，
对全部规上工业出口交货值增长的贡献率居各大类行业前三位，合计达
66.7%，说明出口对工业生产的拉动并未明显减弱，且支撑力量主要集中在装
备制造和电子产业链。综合来看，年内工业增加值增速或温和放缓，结构升级
趋势明确，高技术制造业和装备制造业是核心支撑。在强出口和稳投资政策发
力下，单月增速有望小幅回升，但难以扭转全年较前三季度小幅放缓的格局。
 整体投资降幅进一步扩大，重点基建领域维持较高景气。2026 年 1-10 月，全
国固定资产投资（不含农户）同比下降 8.1%，降幅较 1-9 月扩大 0.9 个百分
点。根据历史数据倒推测算，10 月单月投资同比下降 17.84%，降幅较上月扩
大 8.16 个百分点。累计降幅扩大相对温和，但单月降幅明显加深，反映近期
投资下行压力仍在加大。基建投资方面，1-10 月广义基建投资同比下降 5.1%，

降幅较 1-9 月扩大 0.9 个百分点；根据历史数据倒推测算，10 月单月广义基建
投资同比下降 17.63%，降幅较上月扩大 7.15 个百分点。资金端来看，10 月
地方政府新增专项债发行 3280 亿元，同比涨幅收窄至 14.13%，发行进度
88.07%，慢于去年同期 2.03 个百分点。专项债发行节奏放缓，叠加前期资金
向项目建设转化存在时滞，基建投资尚未形成回升趋势。尽管广义基建投资整
体负增长，但重点领域投资仍保持较快增长。1-10 月互联网和相关服务业投资
同比增长 48.4%，增速较 1-9 月份加快 1.7 个百分点，反映数字基础设施建设
仍有较强投资需求；航空运输业、水上运输业投资均增长 12.5%，电力供应业
投资增长 9.9%，说明“两重”项目建设及“六张网”相关领域投资仍在推进。
往后看，在四季度财政资金集中拨付、重大项目加速形成实物工作量的支撑下，
11、12 月单月投资增速环比有望小幅改善，带动全年降幅较 1-10 月略有收窄，
但全年转正的概率较低。基建投资中部分结构性高增领域可能受低基数、项目

集中开工影响，持续性需观察。同时，专项债剩余额度在年内可能对基建形成
一定支撑，但不宜高估其扭转深度负增的能力。
 地产供需两端低位运行，投资意愿与在建规模仍偏弱。2026 年 1-10 月，全国
房地产开发投资同比下降 21.6%，降幅较 1-9 月扩大 1.0 个百分点；据历史数
据倒推测算，10月单月房地产开发投资同比下降 34.24%，降幅较上月扩大 7.04
个百分点，地产对整体投资的拖累有所加深，是投资端最大拖累项之一。供给
端，根据历史数据倒推测算，10 月房屋施工面积同比下降 32.5%，降幅较上
月扩大 13.9 个百分点，反映在建规模明显收缩；新开工面积同比下降 18.0%，
降幅收窄 8.1 个百分点，是供给端唯一边际改善项。新开工绝对增速仍深度负
增，且可能受低基数、项目集中开工影响，房企投资意愿仍未实质性修复；竣
工面积同比下降 39.1%，降幅扩大 2.4 个百分点，反映项目交付端压力或边际
上升。需求端，根据历史数据倒推测算，10 月商品房销售面积同比下降 20.7%，

降幅较上月收窄 1.9 个百分点；销售额同比下降 20.5%，降幅收窄 0.6 个百分
点。销售额降幅收窄幅度小于销售面积，意味着销售均价同比表现相对偏弱，
销售端的量价修复仍不充分。同时，销售端边际改善与投资、施工、竣工端恶
化形成背离。说明销售回款尚未有效转化为房企拿地、开工和施工强度，房企
流动性和投资意愿仍是核心约束。资金端，据历史数据倒推测算，10 月单月
房地产开发资金来源同比下降 22.0%，降幅较上月进一步扩大。其中国内贷款、
自筹资金、定金及预收款分别下降 33.0%、22.0%、15.6%，资金链仍在全面

3
收缩。往后看，年内稳地产政策以公积金改革、核心城市限购优化、购房贴息
等需求端工具为主，供给端主要依赖收储去库存，年底地产投资降幅仍在寻顶，
降幅收窄时点或推迟到 2027 年。
 整体企业资本开支意愿偏弱，高技术制造投资稳健增长。2026 年 1-10 月，制
造业投资同比下降 2.9%，降幅较 1-9 月扩大 0.8 个百分点；据历史数据倒推
测算，10 月单月制造业投资同比下降 9.32%，降幅较上月扩大 8.38 个百分点。
结合本月工业生产数据看，当前生产端的增长尚未充分转化为企业新增资本开
支，制造业投资意愿相对谨慎。分行业看，1-10 月高技术制造业投资同比增长
3.0%，其中电子电路制造业投资增长 59.6%，锂离子电池、集成电路制造业
投资分别增长 18.0%、13.8%；高技术服务业投资增长 5.9%，其中与人工智
能等新兴产业密切相关的信息服务业投资增长 20.5%，显示科技产业投资持续
向电子信息制造和人工智能相关服务领域集中。“两新”政策持续落地显效，

设备购置投资规模不断扩大。1-10 月，设备工器具购置投资同比增长 8.9%；
占全部投资的比重为 20.3%，比上年同期提高 3.2 个百分点。10 月单月，金属
制品业、有色金属冶炼及压延加工业投资增速同比分别下降 8.5%和 2.5%，降
幅较上月分别收窄 8.2 和 6.8 个百分点。铁路、船舶、航空航天和其他运输设
备制造业、汽车制造业、通用设备制造业当月投资同比分别下降 0.2%、21.8%、
21.2%，降幅分别扩大 12.7、13.6、23.5 个百分点。装备制造业内部投资分化
加剧，终端需求偏弱、价格竞争加剧、产能利用率不足，以及前期抢出口和“以
旧换新”带来的需求前置，可能共同影响企业扩产意愿，促使企业资本开支趋
于谨慎。对比来看，10 月装备制造业工业增加值仍增长 10.6%，反映出当前
装备制造业呈现生产保持较强韧性、投资扩张意愿不足的特征，生产端与投资
端的背离进一步加深。往后看，高技术制造业投资动能有望延续，四季度制造
业投资单月同比有望转正，但全年增速明显转正难度较大；设备购置、电子电

路、锂电、集成电路和 AI 相关信息服务是主要支撑，汽车、通用设备和传统
制造或成为主要拖累。
 消费总量增长继续放缓，部分品类受到价格和低基数扰动。10 月单月，社会
消费品零售总额同比增长 0.4%，增速较上月收窄 0.7 个百分点；扣除汽车后
的消费品零售额同比增长 2.5%，增速较上月收窄 1.4 个百分点。分项看，商
品零售同比增长 0.3%，增速较上月收窄 0.7 个百分点；餐饮收入同比增长
0.7%，增速收窄 1.4 个百分点，整体消费需求仍偏弱。分品类看，汽车类零售
额同比下降 21.4%，降幅较上月扩大 0.2 个百分点，成为社零增长的主要拖累，
反映居民大额耐用品消费意愿不足。通讯器材类零售同比增长 6.5%，增速较
上月大幅回落 24.6 个百分点，说明前期高增长后基数效应明显，补贴效应和
换机需求均有所减弱。金银珠宝类零售同比下降 28.7%，降幅较上月扩大 19.1
个百分点，主要因近期金价回调压低零售额读数，价格因素大于需求因素，也
反映出金银珠宝消费对金价波动高度敏感。石油及制品类零售同比由下降转为

上升 4.4%，主要受近期油价上涨叠加基数因素影响。家用电器和音像器材类
零售同比上升 2.5%，家具类零售同比下降 2.6%，增速均边际回升，或受去年
“以旧换新”退潮带来的低基数影响。尽管消费总量增速有所回落，消费结构
升级和服务消费韧性仍在延续。1-10 月，社会消费品和服务零售总额同比增长
2.5%，其中服务零售额增长 4.9%，高于商品零售额 4.0 个百分点。往后看，
以旧换新政策边际效应减弱、居民消费意愿回升偏慢等因素或持续拖累社零整
体增速。年内消费韧性更多取决于服务消费能否延续较快增速，以及即时零售、
银发经济等新增长点能否对冲传统商品消费偏弱的拖累，托举社零中枢维持在
温和区间。
 风险提示：政策落地节奏不及预期风险，海外经济超预期波动风险。已公布的
宏观或产业政策在实际执行过程中，可能因配套措施不完善、部门协调不畅、
地方执行力度差异或市场环境变化等因素，导致其推进速度、覆盖范围或最终
效果慢于或弱于市场预期，从而影响政策目标的实现与经济修复的进程；主要
经济体的政治局势、经济数据或宏观政策出现意外变化，其幅度或频率超出市

场普遍预测。这种超预期波动可能引发全球金融市场动荡、贸易条件恶化或外
需急剧收缩，并通过金融、贸易及预期渠道向国内传导，增加我国外部环境的
复杂性与不稳定性。

本报告署名分析师具有中国证券业协会授予的证券投资咨询执业资格并注册为证券分析师，报告所采用的数据均
来自合法合规渠道，分析逻辑基于分析师的职业理解，通过合理判断得出结论，独立、客观地出具本报告。分析师承
诺不曾因，不因，也将不会因本报告中的具体推荐意见或观点而直接或间接获取任何形式的补偿。

投资评级说明 
报告中投资建议所涉及的评级分为公司评级和行业评级（另有说明的除外）。评级标准为报告发布日后 6 个月内
的相对市场表现，即：以报告发布日后 6 个月内公司股价（或行业指数）相对同期相关证券市场代表性指数的涨跌幅
作为基准。其中：A 股市场以沪深 300 指数为基准，新三板市场以三板成指（针对协议转让标的）或三板做市指数（针
对做市转让标的）为基准；香港市场以恒生指数为基准；美国市场以纳斯达克综合指数或标普 500 指数为基准。 
公司评级 
买入：未来 6 个月内，个股相对同期相关证券市场代表性指数涨幅在 20%以上 
持有：未来 6 个月内，个股相对同期相关证券市场代表性指数涨幅介于 10%与 20%之间 
中性：未来 6 个月内，个股相对同期相关证券市场代表性指数涨幅介于-10%与 10%之间 
回避：未来 6 个月内，个股相对同期相关证券市场代表性指数涨幅介于-20%与-10%之间 
卖出：未来 6 个月内，个股相对同期相关证券市场代表性指数涨幅在-20%以下 
行业评级 
强于大市：未来 6 个月内，行业整体回报高于同期相关证券市场代表性指数 5%以上 
跟随大市：未来 6 个月内，行业整体回报介于同期相关证券市场代表性指数-5%与 5%之间 
弱于大市：未来 6 个月内，行业整体回报低于同期相关证券市场代表性指数-5%以下

GS Factor Profile  ______________________________

ZDT 3Q26 core business (OPI) was 10%/11% higher than GSe and 
BBG consensus, mainly thanks to the better than expected product 
mix (high margin & high growth sectors accounted for 27% of total 
revenue in 3Q26 vs. our estimate only at 20-25%). In 3Q26, ZDT GM 
was 1.8/2.6ppt higher than GSe/BBG consensus, and was 27.7% up 
4.6ppt QoQ and 5.7ppt YoY, while the annual growth was only 
driven by the better product mix/ new business contribution 
(ABF/optical module/AI PCB), even considering these new 
businesses only accounted for 27% of total revenue.  
OPEX in 3Q26 increased 24% QoQ and 56% YoY due mainly to the 
higher investment in the R&D projects for future new high-end AI 
PCB/ optical transceiver / substrates business, which was the main 
reason for the company to see a 0.7/1.4ppt higher than GSe/BBG 
consensus OPEX to sales ratio in 3Q26.  
In 4Q26, we expect ZDT revenue to go up by ~29% QoQ, mainly due 
to the solid growth from the optical transceiver, substrates, and AI 
PCB businesses, with more new AI projects to ramp in 4Q26. 
Moreover, we expect the company’s GM to further expand to ~30% 
(2.2ppt QoQ or 7.3ppt YoY) thanks to a better product mix (we 
expect high growth/high margin new business will account for 
30-35% of the company’s total revenue in 4Q26, whose average GM 
should be at least 40-45% in 4Q26).  
Investment view 
We remain positive on ZDT’s AI business outlook, and expect the 
acceleration on ZDT’s AI PCB/Substrates business revenue to further 
drive the company’s overall GM level in coming years (we expect the 
two businesses’ average GM could surpass 50% in 2027, thanks to 
the favorable supply demand condition) while we also expect the 
high GM optical transceiver (50%+ GM now) will also see at least 
120% YoY growth in 2027, suggesting a significant GM expansion 
trend for the overall company in coming quarters/years.

### Page 3

We believe ZDT will continue to be the key share gainer in the AI PCB market. For AI GPU 
projects, we are seeing ZDT share increase from <5% in 2025 to ~10-15% in 2026, 
which, we believe, will further go up to 30%+ in long term (2028 and beyond), thanks to 
the company’s proactive investment in the R&D on the latest PCB technology, as well as 
the company’s solid investment in the AI grade PCB capacity. For AI ASIC projects, the 
company’s market share is well below 10% for now, and only focus on some minor 
projects, but we do expect the company to become one of the major suppliers for AI 
ASIC projects in coming years, considering the company’s more diversified production 
sites (mainland China + Taiwan + Thailand), while most of the AI ASIC customers are 
asking for non-Greater China capacity now.  
For the optical transceiver business, we expect ZDT to see strong growth (50%+ 
2025-29E CAGR), driven mainly by the ~50% 2026-28E volume demand CAGR and the 
company’s proactive expansion in capacity in coming years (5x more capacity over the 
next two years). Moreover, ZDT currently holds an industry-leading yield of 90%+ for its 
optical module mSAP products (vs. peers only at 60-80% per our industry checks).  
For its ABF substrate business, we believe the company will continue to have the ability 
to increase ABF price, supported by full UTR and strong demand, while the industry in 
our view remains conservative on its capacity expansion plans over the next 1-2 years 
relative to our forecasts. We expect ABF substrate LTA/Spot prices to increase by 
10-15%/20%+ QoQ per quarter from 3Q26 through at least the end of 2027, with 
shortages likely extending into 2028 (see here). Overall, the business condition for ABF 
substrate is improving with price increase and we expect the business, which was loss 
making in 2025, to expand its GM to 40%+/50%+ by end of 2026/27. 
Overall, we reiterate our Buy rating on ZDT, with a new 12m TP of NT$1,070 (from 
NT$1,025).

Earnings revisions 
We revise up our 2026/27/28/29E net income estimate by 6/21/42/49% after seeing 
the solid GM expansion from these high-growth sectors, which we believe will continue 
to be the net income driver in the coming years. We believe the high growth sectors (AI 
PCB, substrates, optical module mSAP) will account for 50%+ of total revenue in 2028 
and further expand to 60%+ in 2029, with a much higher than company average GM 
(50%+ in average in 2027-28), thus we revise up our 2027/28/29E GM by 
5.4/10.7/12.4ppt vs. previous estimate.

### Page 4

Valuation 
We revise up ZDT 12m TP from NT$1,025 to NT$1,070, still based on our SOTP based 
valuation methodology of (1) 3.5x blended P/B for ABF & BT substrate (in-line with 
upcycle industry average), (2) 15x P/E for high-growth AI server/switch related PCB (in 
line with industry average), and (3) 8x P/E for low-growth consumer electronics PCB 
(in-line with industry downcycle average valuation), and maintain our valuation period at 
4Q26-3Q27, factoring in our earnings revision.

Zhen Ding Technology (ZDT) is the global leading PCB supplier (top 1 in market share in 
sales) with 7-10% market share, and started its ABF substrate business from 2023 in 
Shenzhen, China, and has the highest ABF revenue exposure to the China market 
compared to all other ABF substrate suppliers. We believe ZDT will hold more than 40% 
ABF substrate market share in China by 2027E (up from 31% in 2024) and benefit the 
most from the strong and growing China ABF substrate market (53% 2025E-27E CAGR) 
due to the increasing in-sourcing ratio for the AI computing ASIC/GPU market in China. 
We also expect the increasing foldable smartphone penetration rate in the long term to 
drive ZDT’s FPC TAM, and account for 50% of the company’s total revenue in 2027E. We 
see further revenue upside potential from foldable phones. We are Buy rated mainly due 
to our positive view on the company’s China ABF substrate business opportunity, which 
should not only drive ZDT’s revenue growth, but also improve its OPM level in the long 
term. We see upside for the stock price considering the company’s solid growth 
opportunity, and our TP implied P/E multiple is still lower than the company’s past 10+ 
year P/E average, which we believe is fair considering the potential upside from China 
ABF substrate market and increasing foldable phone penetration rate.

Valuation: Our 12-month TP of NT$1,070 is based on a SOTP valuation methodology,
including (1) 3.5x blended 2028E P/B for ABF & BT substrate, (2) 15x 2028E P/E for
high-growth AI server/switch related PCB (in line with industry average), and (3) 8x
2028E P/E for low-growth consumer electronics PCB (in line with industry downcycle
average valuation).
Key downside risks include: (1) weaker-than-expected iPhone demand or slower
content upgrade, (2) delay in new substrate capacity or slower-than-expected yield rate

ramp up progress, and (3) fiercer competition in China ABF market.

Ratings, coverage universe and related definitions
Buy (B), Neutral (N), Sell (S) Analysts recommend stocks as Buys or Sells for inclusion on various regional Investment Lists. Being assigned a Buy or Sell
on an Investment List is determined by a stock’s total return potential relative to its coverage universe. Any stock not assigned as a Buy or a Sell on an
Investment List with an active rating (i.e., a  stock that is not Rating Suspended, Not Rated, Early-Stage Biotech, Coverage Suspended or Not Covered), is

deemed Neutral. Each region manages Regional Conviction Lists, which are selected from Buy rated stocks on the respective region’s Investment Lists
and represent investment recommendations focused on the size of the total return potential and/or the likelihood of the realization of the return across
their respective areas of coverage.  The addition or removal of stocks from such Conviction Lists are managed by the Investment Review Committee or
other designated committee in each respective region and do not represent a change in the analysts’ investment rating for such stocks.

Total return potential represents the upside or downside differential between the current share price and the price target, including all paid or
anticipated dividends, expected during the time horizon associated with the price target. Price targets are required for all covered stocks. The total
return potential, price target and associated time horizon are stated in each report adding or reiterating an Investment List membership.

ASMedia Technology Inc  | Asia Pacific
Inventory correction extends
into 1Q27
Inventory adjustment extends into 1Q27. Management sees improving
motherboard sell-through, but expects customer destocking to constrain shipments
in 4Q26–1Q27. AM4 exceeded 70% of chipset shipments in 3Q and should remain
dominant in 4Q, weighing on ASPs. Management indicated GM of around 50% in
4Q26–1Q27 as foundry and backend costs rise; alternative packaging/testing sources

should begin helping from 2Q27. October's stronger proprietary mix provided some
relief, but 2027 revenue guidance remains unfinalized, with 20% growth now
considered aggressive.
Recovery likely depends on execution from 2H27. ASMedia signed a new SOW
with the customer and currently sees itself as the sole supplier. Management hopes
shipments will start in 2Q27, with meaningful platform volume from 3Q27; ASPs
would be at least 50–60% above AM4. PCIe switches contributed 6–8% of revenue

after Gen4 yield and thermal issues delayed design-ins, versus a double-digit mix
target for 2027. USB4 is expected to more than double from 2026's depressed base,
although robotics remains small. New products can support recovery, but higher
development spending limits near-term operating leverage.

Valuation Methodology and Risks
ASMedia Technology Inc (5269.TW)
Base case, residual income model. Key assumptions: cost of equity of 9.8% (beta 1.3, risk-free
rate 2% and risk premium 6%), medium-term growth rate of 8.0%, payout ratio of 75% and
terminal growth rate of 5.2%.
Risks to Upside
n AMD gains market share thanks to better-than-expected performance.
n In-house designed products grow faster than expected.
n US-China tensions improve and US tariffs alleviate.

Risks to Downside
n AMD cannot get substrate supply, causing earnings downside for ASMedia.
n Intel adopts TSMC's technologies more aggressively to compete with AMD.
n Spec migration on USB 4 is slower.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views

below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

All Ring Tech Co. (6187.TWO): CoWoS expansion to drive growth ahead; 
Oct rev on track; 3Q26 OP income in line; Buy
12 November 2026 | 7:57AM HKT
Oct revenues on track: Oct revenues came in at NT$978mn, +218% YoY but -16% 
MoM (vs. 3Q26 revenues +41% QoQ), accounting for 33% of our 4Q26E revenue 
forecast. Looking into 4Q26, we expect revenue to decline 11% QoQ, yet 24% ahead 
of our prior forecast on stronger-than-expected CoWoS related demand, while we 
lower our GM assumption to 50.6% (vs. 52.6% previously) to reflect a less favorable 
product mix and early stage shipments of new tools for panel level packaging and 
CPO. For 2027, we expect continued CoWoS capacity expansion at both foundry and 
OSAT customers, rising content value in panel level packaging and the ramp of the 
new CPO product line to drive growth, with GM set to recover as new products reach 
scale. Maintain Buy.

3Q26 operating income in line: Revenue was pre-announced. GM of 48.5% came in 
below GSe/Bloomberg consensus of 54.9%/54.3%, which we attribute to product 
mix, with a higher contribution from lower-margin automation equipment. However, 
with a larger revenue scale and a lower opex ratio of 15.6% (vs. GSe/cons. of 
18.2%/17.7%), operating income grew 39% QoQ, 2% / 1% behind GSe/cons., largely 
in line. Below the operating line, All Ring booked a non-operating loss of NT$55mn in 
3Q26 (vs. non-operating income of NT$174mn in 2Q26), primarily attributable to 
valuation losses from equity investment, partly reversing the gains booked in 2Q26, 
which led EPS (NT$8.49) to miss by 10% vs GSe.

### Page 2

CoWoS expansion and new product ramps to drive growth: We expect 4Q26E 
revenue to decline 11% QoQ, still well ahead of our prior forecast on CoWoS-related 
demand. Our forecast implies average monthly revenue of NT$983mn in 
November-December (broadly flat vs. October). On the margin front, we expect GM to 
remain subject to product mix, with new tools carrying lower margins in the initial ramp. 
Longer term, we remain positive on All Ring, and expect 2027-28E growth to be driven 
by 1) continued CoWoS capacity expansion at both foundry and OSAT customers; 2) 
panel-level packaging, which brings higher-ASP tools and additional process steps, 
lifting content value per line; 3) the ramp of its new CPO product line, spanning fiber 
coupling and optical engine inspection tools, which we expect to start contributing 
meaningfully in 2027; and 4) new customer penetration, including a US customer’s 
advanced packaging capacity.

Earnings revision: We incorporate the 3Q26 results into our model and revise our 
2026/2027/2028E EPS by +2.7%/0.0%/+1.7%, as 1) higher revenue 
(+9.4%/+1.4%/+3.6% vs. our prior forecasts) on stronger CoWoS-related demand and 2) 
a lower opex ratio on the larger revenue base are offset by 3) lower GM, reflecting the 
product mix and the initial ramp of new products, and 4) the non-operating loss in 
3Q26. Net net, we expect All Ring’s revenue to grow 87%/33%/29% YoY in 2026-28E 
(vs. 71%/44%/26% previously), with GM of 51.0%/53.1%/53.5% (vs. 
53.8%/54.2%/54.9% previously).

Valuation: We maintain our 12-month TP at NT$1,450 (implied 35x 2027E PE vs. 36% 
NI YoY of 2027-28E on avg.), still based on a target P/E multiple of 35x (unchanged; 
derived by applying the implied earnings growth uplift (per GSe) to the company’s 
historical valuation framework) applied to our FY27E (unchanged) EPS. We view CPO as 
an even stronger structural tailwind for All Ring than CoWoS, with the potential to drive 
multiple-fold earnings expansion over the next several years, further supporting both 
earnings visibility and scope for valuation re-rating. Maintain Buy.

### Page 1

M
Update
Compal Electronics  | Asia Pacific
Server Ramp Takes Shape but
Valuation Keeps Us UW
Compal’s server expansion is progressing, with revenue
contribution rising to ~12% in 3Q and management targeting 30-
40% in CY27. However, weaker PC volumes and diminishing
scale benefits continue to pressure margins. At ~14x CY27E P/E,
valuation remains stretched.
3Q OpM missed. See our  3Q26 Results Summary                   .
PC business facing demand and profitability headwinds: Compal expects a mid-

teens q/q decline in 4Q26 shipments because of higher selling prices. This weakness
should persist into CY27; the company projects a likely double-digit y/y unit decline,
though it admitted that visibility remains low. However, rising ASPs from mix
changes and cost pass-through could keep 4Q PC revenue up sequentially.
Servers have become a key growth driver: Servers rose from ~5% of the mix in 1Q
to ~12% in 3Q, with a CY27 revenue contribution target of 30-40%. While 4Q26

server revenue is guided to be flat q/q, Compal is aggressively scaling its capacity. It
now primarily ships from Taiwan, and a major L10/11 ramp will begin in 2Q27 at its
new Texas plant. It currently does L10 HGX for Neocloud customers and L6 for a US
enterprise customer; it will do L10/11 for enterprise customer(s) as well, and is
actively approaching CSP customers for ASIC L11 solutions as well.
Margins to remain pressured: Overall margins were pressured by the PC segment,

driving a sequential decline in 3Q owing to lower PC volumes and utilization. This
margin headwind should intensify in 4Q because of shrinking PC business scale.
Conversely, server gross margin should remain resilient in the high single digits to
10%,  staying flat q/q.
4Q preview: We estimate that 4Q revenue will grow ~8% q/q, to NT$263bn (+38%
y/y), primarily driven by higher PC ASPs despite declining volumes. GM should
contracting ~10bps q/q, to 4.3% (-150bps y/y), with declining PC profitability, mainly

owing to diminishing scale benefits. This leads to our OP estimate of NT$2.8bn
(+25% q/q, +6% y/y) and NP estimate of NT$1.7bn (-34% q/q, +21% y/y), for EPS of
NT$0.39. Our 4Q net profit estimate is down q/q because of lower non-op income.
Stay UW with price target unchanged at NT$23.0 (10.4x CY27 P/E). We think
valuation looks quite stretched now at ~14x CY27e P/E. We prefer other ODMs like
Wistron (6x), Hon Hai (12x), Wiwynn (8x), or Quanta (9x).

Morgan Stanley appreciates your support in
the 2027 Extel Asia Research Survey.

### Page 4

4
Price Target and Valuation Methodology
Our 12-month price target is unchanged at  NT$23.0: Our price target is our base case
scenario value, derived from a multistage residual income (RI) model. We use the
company's beginning equity plus the present value of all expected residual income in
excess of the cost of capital. The RI is positive when ROAE is above the cost of capital and
negative when it is below.
We assume a cost of equity of 9.1%  (beta of 1.3, equity risk premium of 6.0% and risk-free

rate of 1.5%), a medium-term growth rate of 5%, and a terminal growth rate of 3% (all
unchanged).
Our new price target implies a 2026e P/E of 10.4x and a 2027e P/E of 8.5x, below the five-
year average of 13.8x. We view these as fair, because we factor in more conservative
assumptions for its consumer business and a weaker margin outlook for Compal. The
company's venture into the "new businesses" (e.g., auto, server, etc.) is constraining its
margin profile as it tries to shift from 80%+ PC to more commercial exposure.

Our bull and bear case values are also unchanged, at NT$38.0 and NT$17.0,
respectively.

▪ Compal’s server expansion is progressing,

with revenue contribution rising to ~12% in
3Q and management targeting 30-40% in
CY27.

diminishing scale beneﬁts continue to
pressure margins.

EPS estimate, below the ﬁve-year average of
14x. We view this implied P/E as fair, because
we factor in more conservative assumptions
for its PC business and a weaker margin
outlook for Compal.

Valuation Methodology and Risks
Hon Hai Precision (2317.TW)
Base case scenario value, derived from a residual income valuation model. Our key assump-
tions include a cost of equity of 8.5%, a medium-term growth rate of 13%, and a terminal
growth rate of 3%.
Risks to Upside
n Better-than-expected iPhone sell-through
n Faster progress in AI server business
n Faster EV business development progress
n Any new M&A activity that could improve sentiment

Risks to Downside
n Lower iPhone sell-through
n Smaller profit contribution from AI business
n Slower EV business development progress
n Geopolitical developments that could negatively affect foreign investment
Quanta Computer Inc. (2382.TW)
Base case, residual income model. Key assumptions include a cost of equity of 9.0% (beta of
1.2, equity premium of 6.0% and risk-free rate of 1.5%), a 7.0% medium-term growth rate, and
a 3% terminal growth rate.

Risks to Upside
n Stronger-than-expected NB demand
n Stronger-than-expected Apple Watch demand
n Stronger-than-expected server demand
n Faster-than-expected AI server penetration
Risks to Downside
n Weaker-than-expected NB demand
n Softer-than-expected Apple Watch demand
n Weak margin performance owing to rising labor costs and sales shortfalls
n Fierce price competition in the mega data center segment
n Slower-than-expected AI server penetration

Wistron Corporation (3231.TW)
Base case, residual income valuation. Key assumptions: 8.7% cost of equity, 5.0% medium-
term growth rate and 3% terminal growth rate.
Risks to Upside
n Faster-than-expected divestiture of consumer electronics business
n Stronger-than-expected NB demand
n Margin expansion from better product mix

Hold or Sell to the stocks we cover. Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated and Underweight are not the equivalent of buy, hold and sell.  Investors should carefully read the definitions of all
ratings used in Morgan Stanley Research. In addition, since Morgan Stanley Research contains more complete information concerning the analyst's views, investors should carefully read Morgan

Stanley Research, in its entirety, and not infer the contents from the rating alone.  In any case, ratings (or research) should not be used or relied upon as investment advice.  An investor's decision
to buy or sell a stock should depend on individual circumstances (such as the investor's existing holdings) and other considerations.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -
relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.

배당수익률 4.1  3.4  3.4  4.1  
ROE 12.4  8.4  6.8  7.7

주가 추이

3Q26 매출액과 영업이익은 비현금성 비용 항목 개선으로 시장 기대치
를 상회했으나, 차량 판매 감소와 인플레이션 등으로 연간 가이던스는 
하향되었습니다. 올해 생산 차질에 따라 이연된 신차 판매를 ’27년에 
확대하며 매출/이익 회복을 가시화하는 부분에 주목 필요합니다. 
매출 성장은 금융부문이, 영업이익은 자동차부문이 기여 
동사 3Q26 매출액은 45.1조원(-3.5% 이하 YoY), 영업이익 2.68조원
(+5.5%)을 기록하며 시장 기대치를 상회했다. 파업으로 인한 생산 차질
로 3Q 판매량 92.1만대(-11.3%)에 그치며 자동차부문 매출은 큰 폭으
로 감소(33조원, -10.0%)했으나, 금융부문 매출이 취급 자산 증가로 큰 
폭으로 성장(+27.8%)했다. 반면, 영업이익은 금리 상승에 따른 금융부문 
영업비용 확대 영향과 기타부문 실적 부진 발생과 달리 자동차부문은 전
년 동기 대비 관세율 차이에 따른 비용 감소와 판매보증 충당부채 전입
액 감소 등 우호적 환율효과로 큰 폭의 증가세(+24.8%) 기록했다. 
’26년 가이던스 하향, 구조적 반등 요인은 아직 
3Q 실적 발표에서 주목할 부분은 가이던스 하향과 현금흐름이다. 연간 
판매목표를 연초 415.8만대에서 400만대로 하향했고 영업이익률 목표
는 6.3~7.3%에서 5.5~6.5%로 0.8%p 하향했으며, ’26년 투자계획은 
17.8조원에서 16조원으로 낮췄다. 특히, 투자계획 하향은 차량 판매 매
출 감소 대비 인센티브, 원자재 가격 상승 등으로 현금흐름이 악화된 
부분에 기인한 것으로 판단된다. 실적발표에서 전사적 원가 최적화 활
동 추진을 강조했으나, 이는 좀 더 장기적 측면에서 효과가 가시화될 
것으로 판단되며, 인플레이션 및 원화절상 상황 고려 시 구조적 반등은 
단기적으로 쉽지 않을 수 있다. 
절치부심의 2027년, 목표주가 600.000원 유지 
그러나 매크로 상황을 제외하면  ’26년 하반기 생산 차질에 따른 신차 
판매효과가 ’27년으로 이연된 것이라는 점에 주목 필요하다.   ‘북미 HEV, 유럽 
BEV’ 전략 하에서 엘란트라, 투싼 풀세인지, GV80, G80 등 고수익 HEV 차종 
중심 미국 수요 대응을 통해 Mix 개선하는 모습이 확인되고, 생산이 본격화된 
아이오닉3 유럽 판매 확대 시 중국 로컬업체들의 공세를 방어하는 모습을 
확인하는 것이 가능할 것으로 판단한다. 노후차종 중심 운영에 따른 판매 둔화 
이외에 아직 근원적 판매 경쟁력 저하로는 판단하지 않는다.

이 자료는 조사분석 담당자가 객관적 사실에 근거해 작성하였으며, 타인의 부당한 압력이나 간섭없이 본인의 견을 정확하게 
반영했습니다. 본인은 이 자료에서 다룬 종목과 관련해 공표일 현재 관련 법규상 알려야 할 재산적 이해관계가 없습니다. 본인은 이 
자료를 기관투자자 또는 제 3 자에게 사전에 제공한 사실이 없습니다. (김성래) 
저희 회사는 공표일 현재 이 자료에서 다룬 종목의 발행주식을 1% 이상 보유하고 있지 않습니다. 
이 자료는 투자자의 증권투자를 돕기 위해 당사 고객에 한하여 배포되는 자료로서 저작권이 당사에 있으며 불법 복제 및 배포를 금
합니다. 이 자료에 수록된 내용은 당사 리서치센터가 신뢰할 만한 자료나 정보출처로부터 얻은 것이지만, 당사는 그 정확성이나 완
전성을 보장할 수 없습니다. 따라서 이 자료는 어떠한 경우에도 고객의 증권투자 결과와 관련된 법적 책임소재에 대한 증빙으로 사
용될 수 없습니다.

### Page 6

현대차 (005380) [한화리서치]

6 
[ 종목 투자등급 ] 
당사는 개별 종목에 대해 향후 1 년간 +15% 이상의 절대수익률이 기대되는 종목에 대해 Buy(매수) 의견을 제시합니다. 또한 절대수
익률 -15~+15%가 예상되는 종목에 대해 Hold(보유) 의견을, -15% 이하가 예상되는 종목에 대해 Sell(매도) 의견을 제시합니다. 밸류
에이션 방법 등 절대수익률 산정은 개별 종목을 커버하는 애널리스트의 추정에 따르며, 목표주가 산정이나 투자의견 변경 주기는 종
목별로 다릅니다.

[ 산업 투자의견 ] 
당사는 산업에 대해 향후 1 년간 해당 업종의 수익률이 과거 수익률에 비해 양호한 흐름을 보일 것으로 예상되는 경우에 Positive(긍정
적) 의견을 제시하고 있습니다. 또한 향후 1 년간 수익률이 과거 수익률과 유사한 흐름을 보일 것으로 예상되는 경우에 Neutral(중립적) 
의견을, 과거 수익률보다 부진한 흐름을 보일 것으로 예상되는 경우에 Negative(부정적) 의견을 제시하고 있습니다. 산업별 수익률 전
망은 해당 산업 내 분석대상 종목들에 대한 담당 애널리스트의 분석과 판단에 따릅니다.

[ 당사 조사분석자료의 투자등급 부여 비중 ] (기준일: 2026 년 9 월 30 일) 
투자등급 매수 중립 매도 합계

### Page 1

(012450) 
한화에어로스페이스 
2026.10.30

AI 로보틱스/방산/조선/운송 양승윤 
6139 / syyang0901@eugenefn.com

여전히 비중확대 의견, 그리고 Top-Pick 
 투자의견 BUY(유지) 
 목표주가 1,660,000 원(유지) 
 현재주가 760,000 원(10/29)

 3Q26 연결 매출액은 6조 943억원(-6.0%yoy), 영업이익은 4,520억원(-
46.2%yoy, OPM 7.4%)을 기록. 영업이익 컨센 1조 1,614억원을 하회.  
 지상방산의 손익이 크게 악화. 영업이익은 217억원(OPM 1.7%) 기록. 주요 
납품이 4분기에 집중된 가운데 일회성 비용 약 500억원(대전사업장 복구·
정상화 약 300억원, 국내 사업 충당금 약 160억원)과 환율 영향이 겹침.   
 다만 내용을 살펴보면 수출 사업의 수익성 훼손은 크지 않았다는 판단임.  
 일회성 제외 영업이익은 약 717억원(OPM 5.8%)이며, 일회성 비용은 대전
사업장이 국내 물량을 전담한다는 점에서 전액 국내 귀속으로 추정. 내수 
OPM을 일회성 제외 -5% 내외로 가정하면, 내수 영업이익은 약 -826억원
(일회성 포함 OPM -12.7%), 수출은 1,043억원(OPM 17.6%)으로 추정됨. 
 1Q26 대비로는 국내 마진 하락이, 2Q26 대비로는 수출 물량 감소가 가장 
크게 작용(도표 참고). 국내는 저마진 사업 매출이 늘고 고마진 반복 양산 물
량이 줄며 일시적으로 믹스가 악화. 수출은 폴란드 등 주요 인도가 정체되어 
매출이 감소한 가운데 저수익 사업 중심으로 믹스가 악화된 영향. 
 4Q26부터 회복 구간 진입 전망. 폴란드 K9 2차 실행계약 물량의 현지 인
도가 본격화되며, 회사는 2026년 폴란드 K9 30문 이상, 천무 발사대 60대 
이상(*연초 40대에서 상향) 인도를 계획. 수출입 데이터상으로도 폴란드·루
마니아향 출하가 대규모로 진행 중이며 폴란드향 부속류 수출도 정상적으로 
이어지는 것으로 확인. 일회성 소멸과 고마진 수출 집중으로 수출 OPM은 
30% 내외로 회복할 것. 다만 국내는 정상 가동(2027년 상반기 목표) 전까
지 한 자릿수 초반 마진에 머물 것으로 전망. 
 중장기 수익성 우려도 크지 않다는 판단. 2027~2029년 매출 연 15% 성
장 가이던스는 유지됐고, 내년 폴란드 K9·천무 인도 물량은 모두 올해 대비 
증가할 계획. 폴란드 사업 이후 높아진 수출 마진 레벨은 후속 사업에서도 
유지될 전망. 수주는 중동 지정학 불안으로 지연되고 있으나 대공체계 등 문
의가 이어지고 있어, 2027년부터 중동이 수주의 중심이 될 것으로 기대. 
 동사에 대한 투자의견 BUY, 목표주가 166만원을 유지함. 3Q26 지상방산 
쇼크는 국내 적자와 수출 물량 공백이 겹친 일시적 결과로, 수출 경쟁력 훼
손으로 보기 어려움. 실적 충격에 따른 주가 조정은 분할 매수 기회로 판단.  
 한화에어로스페이스의 주가는 4월 7일 사상 최고가 153.7만원에서 10월 
29일 76.0만원으로 -51% 조정 받은 상황. 12MF P/E 기준 14.3x, 27E 
P/E는 13.9x로 밸류에이션 관점에서도 매력적인 구간 진입. 그동안 3분기 
실적 우려 등으로 부진했다면, 이제는 실적 바텀아웃과 대형 수주 랠리 속 
다시 한 번 반등을 기록할 차례. 동사를 방산업종 Top-pick으로 유지함.

Compliance Notice 
당사는 자료 작성일 기준으로 지난 3 개월 간 해당종목에 대해서 유가증권 발행에 참여한 적이 없습니다 
당사는 본 자료 발간일을 기준으로 해당종목의 주식을 1% 이상 보유하고 있지 않습니다  
당사는 동 자료를 기관투자가 또는 제 3 자에게 사전 제공한 사실이 없습니다 
조사분석담당자는 자료작성일 현재 동 종목과 관련하여 재산적 이해관계가 없습니다 
동 자료에 게재된 내용들은 조사분석담당자 본인의 의견을 정확하게 반영하고 있으며, 외부의 부당한 압력이나 간섭 없이 작성되었음을 확인합니다 
동 자료는 당사의 제작물로서 모든 저작권은 당사에게 있습니다 
동 자료는 당사의 동의 없이 어떠한 경우에도 어떠한 형태로든 복제, 배포, 전송, 변형, 대여할 수 없습니다 
동 자료에 수록된 내용은 당사 리서치센터가 신뢰할 만한 자료 및 정보로부터 얻어진 것이나, 당사는 그 정확성이나 완전성을 보장할 수 없습니다. 따라서 어떠
한 경우에도 자료는 고객의 주식투자의 결과에 대한 법적 책임소재에 대한 증빙자료로 사용될 수 없습니다

F I R S T  T O  M A R K E T

CTCP VÀNG BẠC ĐÁ QUÝ PHÚ NHUẬN (PNJ)  
Cập nhật ĐHĐCĐ bất thường 21/10/2026

Tái cơ cấu để xử lý áp lực thanh khoản  
— ĐHĐCĐ bất thường PNJ ngày 21/10/2026 đã thông qua điều chỉnh kế hoạch kinh 
doanh và phương án phát hành riêng lẻ để bổ sung vốn tái cấu trúc. Ban lãnh đạo đặt 
ra các vấn đề trọng yếu là đáp ứng nhu cầu thanh khoản và chuẩn bị nguồn lực để thực 
hiện các cam kết thu đổi đã tích lũy trong quá khứ. Bên cạnh đó, các cổ đông tham dự 
cũng đặt các câu hỏi liên quan đến tình hình hoạt động qua các điểm chính (1) dòng 
tiền hoạt động kinh doanh; (2) diễn biến thị trường sau khi PNJ áp dụng chính sách thu 
đổi mới; (3) hiệu quả hoạt động sau tái cấu trúc.

Nội dung chính tại đại hội 
— Theo Ban lãnh đạo, thông tin liên quan đến P-Lab từ đầu tháng 7 khiến khách hàng gia 
tăng bán lại sản phẩm. Tổng giá trị thu đổi và mua lại trong tháng đạt gần 6,000 tỷ VND, 
so với khoảng 7,000 tỷ VND trong cả năm 2025. Theo BCTC riêng, nguồn tiền khả dụng 
giảm từ khoảng 3,700 tỷ VND cuối Q2/2026 xuống 770 tỷ VND cuối Q3/2026, đã bao 
gồm khoản vay 700 tỷ VND từ Chủ tịch HĐQT. Tỷ trọng kim cương và trang sức kim 
cương trong tổng tồn kho tăng từ 41% lên 52%. 
— Q3/2026 ghi nhận lỗ sau thuế 258 tỷ VND trước phần tăng trích lập dự phòng. Để giảm 
áp lực thanh toán, PNJ kéo dài thời gian chi trả đối với kim cương lên 120 ngày từ tháng 
07/2026. Thay đổi chính sách cũng ảnh hưởng đến niềm tin khách hàng và sức mua, cụ 
thể doanh thu và lợi nhuận gộp Q3/2026 đạt lần lượt 6,995/609 tỷ VND, giảm 14%/62% 
YoY. Chi phí bán hàng và quản lý giảm 13% YoY chưa đủ bù đắp mức giảm lợi nhuận 
gộp, khiến PNJ lỗ sau thuế 258 tỷ VND, so với lãi 496 tỷ VND trong Q3/2025.

Biểu đồ 1. Kết quả kinh doanh theo quý  Biểu đồ 2. Diễn biến doanh thu thuần/ngày và doanh thu bán 
lẻ/ngày theo quý

Nguồn: PNJ, KBSV   Nguồn: PNJ, KBSV

### Page 2

2

F I R S T  T O  M A R K E T 
 — PNJ nâng giả định tỷ lệ thu đổi trên giá trị thu đổi còn lại lên 80%, so với 28.8% đối với 
nhóm sản phẩm giai đoạn 2020–2026 trong ước tính tại BCTC bán niên 2026. Tổng dự 
phòng tổn thất dự kiến tăng từ 2,267 tỷ VND lên 7,071 tỷ VND. Kế hoạch điều chỉnh ghi 
nhận doanh thu 39,057 tỷ VND, lỗ trước thuế 6,059 tỷ VND và lỗ sau thuế 6,271 tỷ VND. 
Theo Ban lãnh đạo, LNST loại trừ dự phòng ước khoảng 800 tỷ VND, chủ yếu nhờ kết 
quả Q1/2026. Khoản dự phòng phản ánh tổn thất ước tính cho toàn bộ kịch bản, không 
đồng nghĩa toàn bộ giá trị sẽ được chi trả ngay trong kỳ. 
— Giá trị cam kết thu đổi tích lũy đối với kim cương và trang sức kim cương tại cuối 
Q3/2026 được PNJ ước tính khoảng 21,000 tỷ VND. Với hàng đã bán, doanh nghiệp dự 
kiến chuyển đổi quyền lợi khách hàng sang sản phẩm và các chương trình ưu đãi. Với 
sản phẩm bán mới, chính sách thu đổi định hướng theo giá thị trường tại thời điểm 
giao dịch, thay cho tỷ lệ cố định trên giá trị hóa đơn, nhằm hạn chế tiếp tục tích lũy 
nghĩa vụ tiềm tàng trong tương lai theo cơ chế cũ.

Phương án bổ sung vốn và tái cơ cấu hoạt động kinh doanh 
— Đại hội thông qua phương án chào bán tối đa 550 triệu cổ phiếu. Nguồn vốn dự kiến 
phân bổ 60% cho kế hoạch kinh doanh gắn với điều chỉnh chính sách thu đổi, 20% bổ 
sung vốn lưu động và phần còn lại bổ sung vốn lưu động và/hoặc trả vay ngắn hạn. 
Nhu cầu vốn Q4/2026 và Q1/2027 lần lượt khoảng 3,100 tỷ VND và 2,800 tỷ VND. Giá và 
khối lượng phát hành thực tế chưa được xác định tại đại hội.  
— Doanh nghiệp dự kiến đóng 25–30 cửa hàng và mở khoảng 10 cửa hàng (có chọn lọc) 
trong năm 2026, tương ứng giảm ròng 15–20 cửa hàng; đồng thời đặt mục tiêu giảm 
20–30% chi phí nhân sự. Cổ tức tiền mặt năm 2026 được điều chỉnh về 0%; cổ tức 2025 
về 10% mệnh giá và đã chi trả. 
— 100% mẫu kim cương được AIGS và Vinacontrol kiểm định độc lập là kim cương thiên 
nhiên, phù hợp thông số 4C. PNJ dự kiến trang bị máy kiểm định tại các cửa hàng trọng 
điểm để khách hàng kiểm tra trực tiếp.

Đánh giá của KBSV 
— KBSV cho rằng huy động vốn từ phát hành riêng lẻ là phương án phù hợp ở thời điểm 
hiện tại trong bối cảnh lãi suất tăng cao, giúp PNJ cân đối thanh khoản và duy trì hoạt 
động liên tục. Tuy nhiên, với khối lượng phát hành lớn và chưa có phương án chi tiết, 
doanh nghiệp sẽ cần thêm thời gian để hoàn thành phương án phát hành và cân đối 
giữa lợi ích của cổ đông và doanh nghiệp.  
— Nhu cầu yếu tại Trung Quốc và cạnh tranh từ kim cương nuôi cấy giá thấp tiếp tục gây 
áp lực lên giá kim cương tự nhiên, khiến PNJ gặp khó khăn trong việc tiêu thụ tồn kho 
nhóm kim cương  và gia tăng tổn thất khi mua lại theo chính sách cũ. Thực tế, tỷ trọng 
kim cương và trang sức kim cương trong tổng tồn kho tăng từ 41% cuối Q2/2026 lên 
52% cuối Q3/2026, với giá trị ước khoảng 9,000 tỷ VND. KBSV cho rằng cần theo dõi (1) 
nhu cầu thị trường trong giai đoạn cuối năm; (2) tốc độ giảm tồn kho và dòng tiền thu 
về từ bán hàng; và (3) giá trị khách hàng bán lại. Diễn biến các yếu tố này sẽ quyết định 
khả năng thu hồi vốn, mức độ phục hồi biên lợi nhuận và nhu cầu trích lập bổ sung 
trong quá trình xử lý. 
— Ước tính bán niên sử dụng tỷ lệ tổn thất 20.3% đối với hàng bán trong giai đoạn 2020–
2026 dự kiến được thu mua lại, thấp hơn mức 28.9% trên các giao dịch mua lại thực tế 
từ 01/07–03/09/2026. Vì vậy, bên cạnh giả định thu đổi 80%, mức tổn thất khi xử lý 
hàng mua lại là yếu tố quan trọng cần theo dõi. Việc chuyển hạch toán dự phòng từ 
SG&A sang giá vốn tại kỳ soát xét bán niên cũng cho thấy áp lực biên lợi nhuận bị co 
hẹp trong quá trình tái cấu trúc về mặt kế toán.

Weekly Economics
U.S. Week Ahead
Existing Home Sales • Thursday
Housing market conditions remain highly unfavorable. Mortgage
rates are the primary headwind, and they have climbed even higher
alongside the rise in longer-term yields. At the same time, low
supply is preventing a broad correction in home prices, leaving
prospective buyers squeezed on two fronts.
We expect that existing home sales slid 3.4% in August to an
annualized rate of 3.92 million. Behind this forecast is ongoing

weakness in mortgage applications and a recent bounce in
mortgage rates, which rose from 6.43% at the beginning of July to
6.66% by month-end.
We do not expect affordability conditions to meaningfully improve
in the near term. Supply is likely to remain constrained while high
mortgage rates discourage mobility. And mortgage rates are apt
to remain elevated amid firm economic growth expectations and
heavy government and corporate debt issuance.

### Page 3

Economics Week Ahead Economics
CPI • Friday
Headline inflation looks to have picked up last month. We estimate
the CPI rose 0.40% in August, with gasoline prices increasing a little
over 4% as renewed tensions in the Middle East pushed oil higher.
Grocery prices also likely rebounded after modestly declining in July.
Outside of food and energy, price pressures appear little changed.
We expect the core CPI to rise 0.23%, essentially matching July's

pace. Core goods inflation should remain firm, led by another solid
increase in used vehicle prices as consumer prices continue to
catch up to the trend indicated at wholesale auctions. Meantime,
household furnishings and other tariff-sensitive categories appear
to be stabilizing after declining earlier this year.
Core services inflation should be little changed with another 0.2%
monthly increase. But underneath the surface, we expect a modest
pickup in travel (led by lodging away from home) to offset slightly

cooler medical care and housing cost growth.
All told, we expect the report to show that while headline inflation
remains influenced by the ebbs and flows of the conflict in the
Middle East, core inflation remains contained. Should our forecast
prove correct, core CPI will ease to 2.4% year-over-year, in line with
its 6-month annualized rate but above the more recent 3-month
annualized pace of 1.7%.

### Page 4

Weekly Economics
G10 Week Ahead
European Central Bank Monetary Policy Meeting •
Thursday
We expect the European Central Bank (ECB) to raise the Deposit
Rate by 25 bps to 2.50% next week. August headline inflation
remained elevated at 3.3% year-over-year, though softer core
and services inflation suggest underlying pressures continue to
moderate. Still, the more hawkish tone of the July minutes and
renewed rise in energy prices should keep policymakers cautious.

The recent escalation in the Middle East has pushed fuel prices
at the pump close to their 2022 peaks, while natural gas storage
remains below historical levels, leaving the Eurozone vulnerable
to further energy price increases. The ECB is unlikely to respond
aggressively if the shock remains confined to energy given the
downside risks to growth. However, a broader rise in food inflation
linked to El Niño would be more concerning, as highly visible prices

could lift inflation expectations and generate second-round effects.
For now, resilient Q3 activity, elevated headline inflation and
contained wage growth support one final hike. The ECB will likely
retain and emphasize its scenario-based outlook in the updated
staff projections given continued uncertainty around the conflict
and energy prices. The policy path beyond September will depend
on whether energy and food pressures persist and begin to spread,

which could leave room for further tightening.

U.K. Monthly GDP • Friday
We expect UK GDP to rise 0.2% month-over-month in July, with
growth of 0.3% on a three-month-over-three-month basis.

Activity likely moderated after June’s strong rebound, though
services remained supportive as the July services PMI rose to 52.1.
Retail sales volumes fell 0.5% month-over-month, while softer
manufacturing activity likely reflected some reversal of earlier
precautionary stockbuilding.
The moderation appears more consistent with payback from
June than a broader deterioration in momentum. August PMIs
remain consistent with Q3 GDP growth of around 0.3% quarter-

over-quarter. Still, the renewed escalation in the Middle East and
associated rise in gas and energy prices adds upside risk to inflation
and could weigh on household demand. Against this backdrop and
with Bank of England hawks continuing to emphasize the need for
tighter policy, we expect the BoE to remain cautious and continue
to look for a rate hike in Q4. -1.0%

### Page 5

Economics Week Ahead Economics
EM Week Ahead
Mexico CPI • Wednesday
We expect Mexico’s August inflation rate to edge higher, with
headline CPI rising to 3.34% year-over-year (0.27% month-over-
month) from 3.26% year-over-year in early August. Part of the
pickup likely reflects less favorable base effects and a smaller
disinflationary contribution from food prices. While agricultural
prices continued to help restrain inflation in mid-August, the drag

was notably less pronounced than in July. Core inflation should
remain elevated at 3.98% year-over-year (0.25% month-over-
month).
While inflation remains within Banxico’s midpoint 3% target,
persistent core and services inflation suggest convergence toward
the midpoint of the target could prove gradual. For now, we
continue to expect Banxico to keep rates on hold, with the policy
rate remaining at 6.50% through the rest of 2026 and into early
2027 as policymakers await clearer evidence that underlying

inflation pressures are easing sustainably. While rate cuts remain
a possibility in 2027, the current inflation backdrop argues for
patience in the near term.

Brazil CPI • Friday
We expect Brazil’s August inflation rate to slow to 4.20% year-over-
year, helped by lower electricity, food and fuel prices. The main
driver should be a temporary reduction in residential electricity bills
stemming from a one-off credit, while recent data already point to
a notable easing in price pressures. Mid-August CPI came in weaker
than expected, with inflation falling to 4.24% year-over-year amid
declines in electricity, food, fuel and airfare prices. Beyond these

temporary factors, restrictive monetary policy continues to weigh
on economic activity, as evidenced by the recent slowdown in Q2
GDP growth. However, underlying inflation risks remain. A still-
tight labor market and government measures aimed at supporting
consumption could keep services inflation sticky, making progress
toward the 3.0% inflation target more gradual.
Overall, the inflation outlook remains consistent with additional
policy easing. We expect COPOM to deliver a 25 bps rate cut in

September, bringing the Selic Rate to 13.75%. Beyond September,
policymakers are likely to proceed cautiously as they assess
incoming inflation data and the impact of the election cycle on
inflation expectations. Our base case remains for a pause after
September, with additional easing more likely in 2027.

We have published our 2027 Macro Outlook: Steady Baseline, Downside Risks. We flesh 
out the Global Market implications and key asset views here, highlighting 10 
investment themes that are likely to dominate price action in 2027. More detailed 
outlooks for the individual asset classes are forthcoming.  
A maturing market cycle: Stable global growth and declining inflation make for 1.
a friendly macro baseline, but risks are to the downside as the market cycle ages. 
The AI investment impulse peaks: AI has contributed more to market gains 2.
than economic growth, so a bigger test for markets than the economy.  
Passing—or dropping—the baton: Equity gains move from AI infrastructure to 3.
other areas, but we see risks if tighter financial conditions thwart the transition 
or markets look further ahead. 
Energy—balancing risk and risk premium: Our baseline is for a gradual decline 4.
in energy prices, but an escalation-driven spike remains one of the largest 
near-term risks to markets. 
Bonds reclaim macro and hedging value: Higher running yield, more paths to 5.
relief, and cheap valuations argue for higher bond allocations, as supply shocks 
give way to demand stresses. 
A more cyclical Dollar, Asia’s revaluing currencies: Lower inflation and less 6.
tech exceptionalism should make for a softer Dollar through 2027, especially 
versus Asia’s revaluing currencies (JPY, CNY). 
Europe & UK—global shocks and local shifts: European markets are under 7.
pressure from global shocks and local concerns, which may persist until the 
French election, keeping EUR downside attractive. 
The stress of financing the boom: Record credit and equity issuance has been 8.
digestible so far, but with discounted pricing needed in credit, and further 
widening in US credit spreads is likely.

### Page 2

EM—resilience and diversification: The case for EM is exposure to high, diversified 9.
and resilient yields in EM fixed income and a regionally diversified earnings mix in EM
equities.
Growth risks and tail hedges: A benign baseline and tech has de-rated, but 10.
diversification benefits from bonds and commodities, and equity index volatility has
scope to rise further.

### Page 3

Markets Outlook 2027: Growing Pains

1. A maturing market cycle 
Stable growth, declining inflation make for a friendly macro backdrop. n
But risks are to the downside, and an ageing market cycle is facing more headwinds. n
Higher rates, wider spreads, and a peaking AI impulse mean the broad asset picture n
is more two-sided ... 
... and argues for more diversified allocations, including bonds and hedges. n
Our headline macro forecasts for 2027 are quite friendly: stable growth, declining 
inflation and peaking policy rates. But as the economic cycle extends, the market cycle is 
increasingly showing its age. For 2026, our view was that even though asset market 
valuations were running ahead of the macro, a positive cyclical backdrop including 
strong investment growth should dominate. While the Iran war has upended many 
forecasts, even without the energy shock the market is running into more conventional 
late-cycle headwinds. Real rates are higher, credit spreads are wider and volatility is 
picking up, especially under the surface. On top of this, the AI impulse, which has been a 
key support to markets through the shocks this year, looks poised to peak in the coming 
year. 
That is likely to prove a bigger headwind to the profit and asset market picture than 
growth, given its larger role in supporting equity markets. Markets have already moved 
some way towards reflecting that the extraordinary surge in profitability in the AI 
infrastructure complex is unlikely to be extrapolated. So a flattening in the gains there 
may be offset by gains further down the waterfall among AI users or other cyclical parts 
of the market, especially if energy prices and rates fall more. But that is a tricky 
balancing act, and further boosts to equity prices are likely to come alongside a higher 
base level of volatility. There are also risks that AI-related drawdowns tighten financial 
conditions more meaningfully, or that energy and policy relief do not arrive on time. 
That leaves the broader risk-asset picture looking more two-sided than it has for several 
years, arguing for more diversified and better-hedged portfolios. 
12 November 2026   3
Goldman Sachs Global Markets Outlook 2027

### Page 4

2. The AI investment impulse peaks 
The AI investment impulse peak is a bigger test for markets than the economy. n
That impulse has been a larger contributor to growth in market value and earnings n
than in GDP. 
Markets have already de-rated AI infrastructure beneficiaries. n
Valuation excesses are less visible, so the focus is on sustainability of record profits. n
Coming into 2026, we expected continued acceleration in AI investment to dominate 
valuation concerns in equities, but likely amid higher volatility and wider credit spreads.  
Although we expect solid AI investment growth to continue, our forecasts imply that the 
investment growth impulse—additional AI investment as a share of GDP—will peak in 
early 2027. From a macro perspective, this should be a manageable event. But it may be 
a bigger test for markets for three reasons. First, the AI impulse has been a much larger 
contributor to growth in earnings and market value than to growth in GDP. Second, 
AI-related equities have taken credit for significant macro productivity gains, especially 
companies benefiting directly from the infrastructure boom, that have made them more 
vulnerable to any signs that we are on a less optimistic path. Third, the point at which 
growth rates peak (a “second derivative” turning point) often matters, even if underlying 
growth rates (the “first derivative”) remain healthy. Moving through that peak allows 
markets to place limits on the upside of an uncertain distribution and, as growth 
decelerates, the period of positive surprises to expectations often ends too, as we may 
be starting to see for the AI capex cycle. 
Markets have already been reflecting these concerns. Since June, we have seen a 
significant de-rating of companies levered to the AI infrastructure theme, which are 
most sensitive to shifts in the investment outlook and had been the biggest market 
beneficiaries in the first half of 2026. Moreover, robust profits, and a rising share of

income going to listed companies, have helped keep both the imbalances and the deep 
valuation excesses that we saw in the late 1990s at bay. The key question in this cycle is 
whether the market is correctly assessing how much of the recent acceleration in profits, 
which has taken S&P 500 earnings to a record high relative to GDP, will prove persistent, 
especially as the investment cycle itself fades. Without the capital inflows from the EM 
crises in 1997 and 1998—or the rate cuts that goods price deflation allowed during that 
tech cycle—increased financing needs have also been putting clearer upward pressure 
on the cost of capital.

3. Passing—or dropping—the baton 
Strong levels of AI investment growth and profitability should limit the pressure. n
And the baton may be passed from AI infrastructure to other market areas. n
But we see risks of a larger aggregate shock if the market looks forward, or other n
areas cannot take up the slack. 
Tighter financial conditions or credit conditions could create a feedback loop. n
Two things give us comfort in terms of how bumpy this transition through the peak of 
the investment cycle will prove to be. The first is that although the investment impulse 
from AI looks poised to peak, growth in both AI investment and earnings should remain 
firmly positive in 2027, especially earlier in the year. We expect S&P 500 earnings growth 
to decelerate from 36% in 2026 to a still-robust 11% in 2027. This may ultimately help to 
limit the de-rating of even the most directly affected areas and should reduce the 
impact on broader growth-sensitive assets, though it should also imply less relief in core 
rates pricing. The second is that the baton may be passed, at least to a degree, from the 
direct beneficiaries of the AI investment boom to other areas of the market. This 
includes other areas of the AI complex, such as the hyperscalers, if monetization paths 
become more visible; companies that may begin to show benefits from AI productivity 
enhancements; or non-AI companies that see some relief as the crowding out of capital

available to them eases. 
The risk is that what could be more of a rotation—and a flattening in the upward trend in 
asset markets—becomes a bigger aggregate shock. This could happen because the 
market begins to look further forward to the peak in the investment share itself (in 2028 
on our forecasts); because the passing of the baton to other sectors does not take place 
as quickly or as smoothly as needed; or because feedback loops from financial and 
credit conditions accelerate more quickly than in our baseline. We think the risk to 
markets is higher than the risk to the economic picture on each front, because the boom 
itself has had its largest impact there. For that reason, the most likely channel through 
which AI worries could lead to broader economic downside is through financial 
conditions themselves.

4. Energy—balancing risk and risk premium 
An escalation-driven oil spike remains one of the largest near-term risks to markets. n
Our baseline is for a gradual decline in energy prices, but the hedge value of energy n
is still high. 
Downside tail to energy prices with clearer/faster resolution. n
Risk premium has fluctuated, so hedge value also varies over time. n
A potential oil price spike driven by another escalation in the war with Iran remains one 
of the biggest near-term risks to the markets and growth picture. Particularly after the 
fall in oil prices so far in November, pricing is vulnerable to any broadening in the conflict 
and new supply disruptions. We would expect that to hurt equities, support the Dollar 
against European currencies and put upward pressure on rates, all else equal. Europe, 
too, faces a precarious winter, given low storage levels in natural gas which leave the 
region vulnerable to risks from both supply and weather. If those risks are avoided, our 
baseline is for a gradual decline in energy prices over the coming year even without full 
resolution of the conflict. Despite the ongoing war with Iran, oil market supply has 
largely been restored (even if refining capacity is limited), but risks of escalation and 
further disruptions have generally led the market to demand a fluctuating but 
meaningful risk premium above where we think the fundamental supply-demand picture 
would leave them.

### Page 7

Our baseline is a gradual recovery in supply and reduction in energy prices, leaving 
energy and core commodity returns more muted. But the risk of more serious 
disruptions is real, and energy prices could move significantly higher in those scenarios 
at a time when equities and, potentially, bonds could both be under pressure. The 
hedging value of the asset class remains high for that reason. The wrinkle is that the risk 
premium in oil has varied substantially and conditions the value of the hedge. When the 
risk premium falls, as it has done more recently, that hedging value is likely to be higher. 
When that risk premium is very high, as it was at the end of October, oil markets are 
more vulnerable to downside tails if news flow is better than expected, and outright 
longs (rather than options) have generally been more risky. The downside tail from faster 
progress towards resolution than we expect (not just in Iran, but in Ukraine too) is one of 
the clearest sources of upside risks for broader markets. In that scenario, energy prices 
could come down more quickly, supporting both bonds and equities.

5. Bonds reclaim macro and hedging value 
Rates moved up in 2026, on oil, hikes, AI spending and fiscal deficits. n
Central bank pricing now too hawkish relative to our baseline. n
Bonds should be a better hedge as supply shocks give way to demand stresses. n
Higher running yield, more paths to relief, and cheap valuations argue for higher n
bond allocations. 
Interest rates moved higher in 2026 after a low point on the eve of the Iran war. The 
resultant surge in energy prices and central bank pricing has been the primary catalyst 
across most DM and EM rates markets, but real rates, especially in the US, have 
continued to move higher as investment demand from the AI build-out interacts with 
sustained fiscal deficits. Despite that backdrop, we think that central bank pricing in 
many economies, including the US and the UK, is too hawkish relative to what is likely to 
be delivered. Even with the energy shock, the underlying inflation story is still fairly 
benign, which means that policy rates are likely to peak at lower levels relative to 
2023-2024 and reverse earlier. The risk to this view is that the energy shock extends 
further or inflation remains stickier and the labour market proves more resilient, as it did 
this year. 
Bonds have been a poor hedge in this environment of energy supply shocks, rate hikes

### Page 8

and real rate pressures from private and public sector spending. But we think the case 
for duration has shifted in a more constructive direction for several reasons. First, on a 
static basis, bond yields are now providing a good running yield and a decent spread to 
cash. Second, valuations are cheap (we see UST 10y yields at 4.6% by end-2027), 
positioning is underweight, and there are paths to some shallow rate relief from current 
levels under our benign baseline scenario, and scope for larger moves if AI spending 
decelerates more quickly. Third, the increase in yields has started to create stresses that 
indicate that a higher level of real rates may be difficult to sustain. Bonds are already 
starting to illustrate their value as a hedge against shocks of these types—with Bunds 
rallying as French sovereign spreads widened and USTs rallying as tech stocks came 
under pressure. Although energy spikes remain a near-term risk, we are more positive 
about bond allocations even after the recent rally, in part because of that hedging value.

6. A more cyclical Dollar, Asia’s revaluing currencies 
After tight ranges in 2026, lower inflation, energy prices make for a softer Dollar. n
But a bigger USD swing could come if US tech exceptionalism erodes. n
JPY is a more attractive hedge with ‘team Japan’ focused on limiting Yen weakness. n
CNY appreciation has further to run given undervaluation and a surging trade n
surplus.  
The broad Dollar is finishing 2026 on the strong side of tight ranges, buoyed by a sturdy 
US nominal growth backdrop, higher energy prices, and a shift to more hawkish Fed 
pricing. Under our baseline forecast, all three impulses should fade: alongside trend 
growth we expect inflation in the US to converge towards target, energy prices should 
decline as the Middle East disruptions gradually normalize and we expect the Fed to 
move from hikes to cuts as we head into late 2027 and early 2028. That should make for

### Page 9

a softer Dollar backdrop, and we have pencilled in a roughly 3% fall in the broad 
trade-weighted Dollar in 2027. 
But the real swing factor for FX is likely to be how the peak in AI-related tech spending 
affects US equity returns. Over the past several years, tech-related macro and market 
outperformance in the US has sucked in equity capital flows from the rest of the world 
and has been the primary factor supporting the Dollar’s premium valuation. Our 
baseline forecasts envisage a more even distribution of returns globally rather than US 
underperformance. But if US tech performance erodes meaningfully, that would likely 
come alongside some cyclical underperformance of the Dollar rather than a traditional 
‘safe haven’ bid, as we have already seen in short periods over the past couple of years. 
Instead, we would expect the undervalued Yen to prove a better haven in that scenario, 
given its typical sensitivity to equities and rates falling together. With Japanese policy 
authorities also now focused on limiting further Yen depreciation and encouraging 
inflows into Japanese assets, we think that the Yen should increasingly be a more 
attractive hedge to pro-cyclical portfolios. We also see scope for the Yuan to continue to 
appreciate gradually as China’s trade surplus keeps growing, and forward markets 
underestimate how far this process can run given the deep undervaluation starting 
point. While domestic demand is also very weak, we have argued that China needs both 
currency appreciation and expansionary policy, two instruments to hit two targets, to 
move towards better external and internal balance.

7. Europe & UK—global shocks and local shifts 
European markets have been under pressure from energy ToT, higher global yields n
and sovereign stresses. 
Even if global shocks abate, local concerns still persist until the French election, n
keeping EUR downside attractive.

### Page 10

UK equities are resilient and defensive, Sterling has continued to strengthen. n
UK Gilts may be clearer outperformers given their highest beta to oil prices. n
European macro markets have had a tough year, primarily reflecting a negative terms of
trade shock, rate tightening without an offsetting investment spending boom, and most
recently the reappearance of sovereign stresses, especially in France. Even with the
repricing, there are still challenges: the energy risks are likely to be most acute going

through the winter, and the underlying fiscal issues in France are unlikely to be
adequately addressed until the French election in the Spring of 2027. As a result, using
the Euro as a funder for other crosses has been a popular position, and the skew of risks
and low carry continue to make downside optionality attractive for the near term. While
Europe remains hostage to global shocks, the main paths to relief also run through those
global channels—energy relief would be most helpful, as would lower yields in the rest of

core markets. Together they would also lessen the immediate pressure on fiscal budgets,
even though the underlying primary deficit would still need to be addressed. Any
slowdown in AI-related spending may also show European assets in a better relative light
given a much smaller share of tech in the region’s equity indices.
In those scenarios, the UK may shine even brighter. Gilts have had the highest beta to oil
prices, so a reduction in those pressures may provide the most relief to UK rates and to

the fiscal picture there. UK equity indices also have less tech exposure than the US
(although they do embed significant energy exposure), and so may also prove more
defensive in an AI-related spending slowdown and any associated market pressures. And
while the currency may also benefit from a softer Dollar backdrop, it is already the most
overvalued currency within our G10 coverage. Much has gone right for the Pound in
recent months—a smooth transition to a new Labour government, a ‘do-no-harm’

budget delivery, the BoE shifting to hikes, and encouraging talk of a closer settlement
with the EU. But we think further gains are likely to be more challenging, with our
monetary policy forecasts most dovish to market pricing in the UK.
12 November 2026   10
Goldman Sachs Global Markets Outlook 2027

### Page 11

8. The stress of financing the boom 
AI investment financing has driven credit and equity issuance to cycle records. n
This has been digestible so far, but with discounted pricing needed in credit that has n
led spreads wider. 
A further widening in US spreads looks likely and a higher chance of meaningful n
widening than meaningful tightening. 
Europe offers more risk premium; mortgages and structured credit give some refuge n
from corporate supply. 
Credit and equity issuance has been hitting fresh cycle records, and the financing needs 
of the AI investment boom mean that those needs are likely to build further in 2027. So 
far, that issuance has—for the most part—been digestible, albeit with discounted 
pricing, and the credit underperformance that we expected in 2026 because of these 
dynamics has only really become clear in the last few months. Balance sheets of the 
major borrowers are strong and while free cash flow positions for the hyperscalers have 
deteriorated sharply, the overall corporate sector is much more balanced. We expect 
continued bifurcation between AI-related issuers and “everything else”, with episodic 
indigestion of jumbo AI debt financing leading to wider spreads for AI-related sectors.  
We expect further widening in index-level credit spreads in 2027, though in our base 
case the scope is fairly modest. The risks around that view are skewed though. It is hard 
to see scenarios where US credit spreads tighten sharply (there is more scope for 
tightening in Europe where the risk premium has increased more), and easier to see how 
spreads could move wider, particularly if economic conditions deteriorate more than we 
expect or if investors become more uncomfortable about the profitability of the AI 
investment boom. We expect modest US HY underperformance (decompression) in our

baseline, but the risk is that problems in the left tail of weaker borrowers create more 
turbulent conditions for broader financing. Mortgages and structured credit markets 
may offer some refuge from the heavy corporate supply. From 1998 to 2000, there was 
sustained widening in credit spreads and substantial decoupling between credit and 
equity markets. Without the large capital inflows and goods price deflation from the EM 
crises in 1997 and 1998, we think that kind of divergence will be harder now.

9. EM—resilience and diversification 
2026 is one more year when EM assets have shown resilience/outperformance in the n
face of external shocks. 
But the flip side is less compelling valuations and lower carry, despite friendly macro. n
So the case for EM investing is exposure to high, diversified and resilient yields in EM n
fixed income ... 
... and a regional and sectorally diversified earnings mix in EM equities.  n
EM assets have again demonstrated impressive resilience in 2026 in the face of shocks 
from high energy prices and the spikes in core rates. EM rates have outperformed core 
rates, EM FX total returns are outpacing G9 and EM equities are on track for a narrower 
but still double-digit year after 30% gains in 2025. The upshot of this resilience and 
outperformance is that in many places valuations are less compelling than before. EM 
local rate spreads are tight relative to core rates, EM credit spreads are the tightest in 
years despite attractive all-in yields, and EM FX carry and valuations are firmly 
middle-of-the-road. 
Even though the baseline macro backdrop looks friendly for EM investing and 
carry-harvesting in 2027, less attractive valuations and carry levels mean that it is about 
gaining exposure to high diversified yields in both hard and local currency fixed income. 
In many cases, such as South Africa, Hungary, Colombia and India, those higher yields 
come with improving fiscal and structural reform stories. Likewise, in the case of EM 
equities, while the major markets are not expensive, much of the value in the headline 
index is an artefact of the surge in AI earnings that will be hard to repeat. Rather, the 
case for EM equity length is also about gaining exposure to a diversified earnings mix.

Goldman Sachs Global Markets Outlook 2027

### Page 13

The EM equity index, especially the less concentrated, equal-weighted flavours, provide 
exposure not just to the tech and AI complex in North Asia, but also to cyclical and 
commodity-related growth in South Africa and LatAm, and to larger domestic demand 
stories such as India. And if we see a larger move lower in core rates or the Dollar than 
our baseline expectations, EM local assets provide upside convexity that is harder to 
replicate elsewhere.

10. Growth risks and tail hedges 
A benign baseline, but growth risks now skew to the downside. n
Diversification benefits from bonds, commodities and equity selection. n
Equity index volatility has scope to rise further. n
Downside in rates, USD/JPY, upside in energy and protection in equity options. n
With a steady economic backdrop, our baseline forecasts for 2027 are for positive 
returns across equities and bonds. But with a maturing market cycle and significant 
uncertainty around core drivers of the outlook, we continue to emphasize the value of 
diversification and hedges to protect against key risks. Some of those risks—from higher 
oil prices or sticky core inflation—are likely to push rates higher. But a growing number—
a sharper reversal in the AI boom, cybersecurity fears, AI-related labour-shedding or 
financial stresses—would push both growth and rate expectations lower. The further we 
move into 2027, the more likely it is that the key risks are positive for bonds, as the 
threats from central bank hikes, energy price spikes and the AI investment tailwind 
recede. Diversification remains valuable in this environment. As we have said, the value 
of bonds as a diversifying asset is returning as the skew of risks shifts. And 
commodities—especially energy—continue to play a hedging role for portfolios, 
particularly when implied risk premia fall as they have recently.  
Alongside diversification, direct hedges are worth considering. Positioning for lower 
rates in the US and Japan, or for jointly lower rates and equities are potential ways to 
protect against deeper downside growth tails. In FX, USD/JPY downside may play a 
similar role, given that it generally performs well when rates and equities are both falling

lower volatility, akin to DM assets in recent years
